So final theorem structure:

**Main Theorem (Reduction Theorem).** Let K ⊂ S³ be a nontrivial L-space knot (i.e., some S³_{p/q}(K) with p/q > 0... convention: nontrivial = not the unknot; unknot: S³_r(unknot) is S³ or lens or S¹×S² — at most... unknot surgeries: lens spaces (prime, 1 summand) and S¹×S² (prime, 1 summand) ✓ so unknot fine, handle in remark). Suppose S³_r(K) is reducible. Then:
(1) r ∈ Z ∪ {±cable slope case}: if K is a cable or torus knot, S³_r(K) is either prime or ≅ L # Y with two summands as described [Moser; Gordon–Luecke].
(2) If K is neither a torus knot nor a cable, then r = 2g(K) − 1 ∈ Z, and if S³_{2g−1}(K) has at least three prime summands, then:
 (a) S³_{2g−1}(K) ≅ L(ℓ1, a) # L(ℓ2, b) # Y, with ℓ1ℓ2 = 2g−1, gcd(ℓ1, ℓ2) = 1, ℓ1, ℓ2 ≥ 2, and Y a non-simply-connected integral homology sphere which is itself an L-space;
 (b) 2g(K) − 1 ≤ b(K);
 (c) ℓ1 + ℓ2 ≤ b(K) + 1 and ℓ1ℓ2 ≤ b(K)(b(K)+2)/4;
 (d) th(K) ≥ t − 1, where t is the smallest prime factor of 2g(K) − 1.
(3) [Greene] If S³_r(K) is a connected sum of lens spaces (any number ≥ 2 of summands... Extra corollary to state: **Corollary.** If K is an L-space knot with 2g(K)−1 a prime power, then every Dehn surgery on K yields a manifold with at most two prime summands. Proof: three summands impossible by ℓ1ℓ2 = 2g−1 with coprime ≥2 factors ⟹ 2g−1 not a prime power ⟹ contradiction with branch 3; branches 1-2 fine. ✓ Also more refined: three summands require 2g−1 to have at least two distinct prime factors AND 2g−1 ≤ b AND (Howie) smallest factor pair sum ≤ b+1 AND th ≥ t−1.

**Theorem 5.1 (Reduction Theorem).** Let K ⊂ S³ be a nontrivial L-space knot. Exactly one of the following holds:
(I) K is a torus knot, and every Dehn surgery on K yields a manifold which is prime or has exactly two prime summands; the latter occurs iff the surgery slope is the slope ±pq, and then S³_{±pq}(K) ≅ L(p,a)#L(q,b) [Moser].

(a) the only slope r for which S³_r(K) can be reducible is r = 2g−1 (up to mirroring) [HLZ15 via 2209.01672];

Structure of Main Theorem (call it Theorem A, "Structure of reducible surgeries on L-space knots"):

Theorem A. Let K ⊂ S³ be an L-space knot, and let M = S³_{r}(K) be obtained by Dehn surgery along a slope r. If M is reducible, then K is nontrivial and, up to replacing K by its mirror, exactly one of the following holds:
(i) [torus] K = T(p,q), pq ≥ 6, r = pq, and M ≅ L(p,a)#L(q,b);
(ii) [cable] K = C_{m,n}(J) is the (m,n)-cable of a nontrivial knot J, m ≥ 2, n ≥ 2, r = mn is the cabling slope, and M ≅ L(m,α)#S³_{±n}(J) — hmm careful "±n": write "M is a connected sum of two prime manifolds, one a lens space and the other a Dehn surgery on J of slope ±n (in particular of order n homology)" — hmm, I'll write M ≅ L(m, α) # S³_{n}(J) "up to orientation conventions" — hmm, is it n or −n... "(with the sign depending on cabling conventions)". OK.
